# All relative phases of two scalar secant bands

Status: complete candidate proof, NOT verifier-accepted. No verifier request
is allocated by the current TASK or master guidance. Every new estimate used
in this document is proved here. Earlier C18/C19 findings are motivation,
not premises. The only accepted fact cited is b98bb807d514e80d for the exact
benchmark definitions. No external theorem or numerical search is used.

## 1. Statement

Set c_sc=13/5 and a_sc=c_sc^(-17). Define

    Hsc_0=(), Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,bsc_j,Hsc_j),
    bsc_j=a_sc c_sc^j, Nsc_j=2^j-1,
    ssc_j=sum Hsc_j=a_sc(c_sc^j-2^j)/(c_sc-2).

Let f be a fixed differentiable convex function on the real line with
1-Lipschitz derivative g, f(0)=0=min f. On the two rays choose arbitrary
constants M_slow,M_fast with 0<M_slow<=M_fast<=1. Either physical ray can
be called slow. Suppose each nonzero queried point on ray sigma satisfies

    M_sigma/c_sc <= |g(x_t)|/|x_t| <= M_sigma.             (1)

The constants, derivative, and initial point can depend on the horizon m.
No relation between the two scales is imposed. The derivative may be
nonperiodic and asymmetric, with any number of knots or repeated visits.
Only the finitely many queried secants are constrained by (1).

Put K_sc=192*27^4 and C_sc=200000*c_sc^3*K_sc^2/a_sc. Then GD under the
complete word Hsc_m, starting from any x_0!=0, satisfies

    2 f(x_(Nsc_m))/x_0^2 <= C_sc c_sc^(-m).              (2)

In particular the supremum over this entire class, divided by the exact
U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ, tends to zero. Each ray's secants must fit in its own band of
width factor13/5, but the two bands can have arbitrary relative scale and
phase. This closes the phase restriction in C19. It is still a restricted
scalar class, not all scalar functions and not unrestricted W.

Within the proof abbreviate c_sc,a_sc,bsc_j,ssc_j by c,a,b_j,S_j. These
are local aliases, unrelated to the Huber slopes c,a in the project glossary.
All matrix products below use maximum and multiplication, not addition.

## 2. Exact two-state upper envelope

For ray sigma put

    d_sigma(j)=(1-b_j M_sigma/c)_+,
    u_sigma(j)=(b_j M_sigma-1)_+,
    B_j=[[d_slow(j),u_fast(j)],[u_slow(j),d_fast(j)]].

Rows denote the output ray, columns the input ray. A scalar step with
queried secant lambda on ray sigma has multiplier1-b_j lambda. Its
same-ray magnitude is at most d_sigma(j), and its opposite-ray magnitude
at most u_sigma(j). A zero coefficient excludes that nonzero transition.

Define (A odot B)_(i,j)=max_k A_(i,k)B_(k,j), and let E_0 be the identity
matrix. The exact recurrence for the relaxed sign-path envelope is

    E_(j+1)=E_j odot B_j odot E_j.                       (3)

Induction by the two intermediate signs in the word decomposition shows
that E_j(output,input) bounds every actual radius ratio. This proves an
upper bound even though independently maximizing its entries need not
produce one legal function. No such realization is asserted.

Let I be the first integer with b_I M_fast>1. Write

    t=b_I M_fast, s=b_I M_slow.

Then I>=18, 1<t<=c, and 0<s<=t. Every checkpoint before I has upper
secant product at most1, so E_I is diagonal. Its entries are

    p_s=p(s), p_f=p(t),
    p(z)=product_(h in Hsc_I)(1-h z/(c b_I)).             (4)

All these factors are positive. With eta=13/8, the sum formula gives
S_I/b_I>=eta: (2/c)^3<1/2 and (2/c)^2<4/5 imply (2/c)^17<1/40.
Consequently

    p(t)/p(s) <= exp[-5(t-s)/8].                         (5)

This follows termwise from log(1-u)-log(1-v)<=-(u-v) for 0<=v<=u<1.
We shall also use, for 0<z<=c,

    p(z) <= exp[-5z/8-z^2/70],  p(z)>1/27.             (6)

For the first inequality, log(1-v)<=-v-v^2/2 and the level counts give

    sum_(h in Hsc_I) h^2/b_I^2
       =[1-(2/c^2)^I]/(c^2-2).

Since 2/c^2<1/3 and I>=18, the coefficient of z^2 in the logarithm is
larger than (26/27)/(2c^2(c^2-2))>1/70. For the lower bound, every
v=h z/(c b_I) is at most1/c, and their sum is at most5/3. Integration
gives log(1-v)>=-v/(1-1/c), so log p(z)>=-65/24>-3. The elementary
factorial-series bound e<3 gives p(z)>1/27.

## 3. A common-band product estimate proved from scratch

For M in (0,1] set

    q_sec(z)=max(1-z/c,z-1),
    P_sec(m,M)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)
                     q_sec(a M c^j)^(2^(m-j-1)).

The empty product is1. We prove

    M P_sec(m,M)^2 <=(60000/a)c^(-m).                    (7)

Let k be the largest integer with y=a M c^k<=13/9. Then k>=17 and
5/9<y<=13/9. The first branch of q_sec holds through k; its second
branch holds afterwards. For m>=k+1, log(1-v)<=-v on levels below k
gives

    log P_sec(m,M)/2^(m-k-1)
      <=-5y/4+log(1-y/c)
          +sum_(ell=1)^(m-k-1)2^(-ell)log(c^ell y-1).    (8)

For m=k+1 this is at most-25/36. Otherwise keep ell=1 exactly, extend
the positive terms ell>=2 to infinity, and replace their logarithms by
ell log c+log y. The result is

    E(y)=-5y/4+(1/2)log[c y(c-y)^2(cy-1)].               (9)

On the indicated interval E''(y)<-1/2. Indeed its three negative terms
are -1/(c-y)^2, -1/(2y^2), and -c^2/(2(cy-1)^2); the last two alone
have total magnitude greater than2/9+c^2/18>1/2. At y=4/5,
E'=5/216 and c y(c-y)^2(cy-1)=113724/15625<(27/10)^2. Since
e>163/60, E(4/5)<log(162/163)<=-1/163. The tangent parabola therefore
gives E(y)<=-1/163+25/46656<-1/200. Thus

    P_sec(m,M)<=exp[-2^(m-k-1)/200]  for m>=k+1.         (10)

For m<=k every factor is at most1 and M<=(13/(9a))c^(-m). For
m=k+J, J>=1, equation(10) bounds M P_sec^2 by
(13/(9a))c^(-m)c^J exp(-2^J/200). As c<4 and z^2 exp(-z)<=1 for
z>=0, this is at most (520000/(9a))c^(-m), proving(7).

## 4. Separated activation: s<=1/c

This case is included to keep the statement independent of unverified C19.
At level I write

    A=p_s^2(1-s/c), D=p_s p_f(t-1), F=p_f^2(1-t/c).

Then E_(I+1)=[[A,D],[0,F]]. At I+1 a fast input must cross and a slow
input cannot cross. Hence

    E_(I+2)=[[A^2(1-s), A max(D(1-s),F(ct-1))],[0,0]].

Its right/left nonzero-entry ratio is at most max(R_1,R_2), where

    R_1=(t-1)/(1-s/c) *exp[-5(t-s)/8],
    R_2=(1-t/c)(ct-1)/[(1-s/c)(1-s)] *exp[-5(t-s)/4].

Both increase with s. R_1 increases with t up to c; R_2 decreases with
t from1, since its logarithmic derivative is
-1/(c-t)+c/(ct-1)-5/4, is decreasing, and equals-1/4 at t=1.
Thus R_1<=(169/90)exp(-18/13)<1 and
R_2<=(169/90)exp(-10/13)<1. The latter inequalities follow respectively
from exp(18/13)>31/13>169/90 and
exp(10/13)>349/169>169/90.

If E_j=[[A_j,R A_j],[0,0]] with R<=1, recurrence(3) preserves R and

    A_(j+1)=A_j^2 max(d_slow(j),R u_slow(j))
           <=A_j^2 q_sec(b_j M_slow).

Its initial A_(I+2) is P_sec(I+2,M_slow). Every later nonzero endpoint
is therefore on the slow ray, with radius at most P_sec(m,M_slow)|x_0|.
Equation(7) supplies the required bound in this case.

## 5. Overlapping activation: one off-diagonal entry controls all levels

Now assume 1/c<s<=t<=c. At level I use all four entries

    E_(I+1)=[[A,D],[C,F]],
    A=p_s^2(1-s/c), D=p_s p_f(t-1),
    C=p_s p_f(s-1)_+, F=p_f^2(1-t/c).

At I+1 put d=(1-s)_+, u_s=cs-1>0, u_f=ct-1>0. Thus
B_(I+1)=[[d,u_f],[u_s,0]]. The two relations

    C d=0,  C u_f<=D u_s                              (11)

hold: for s<=1 the first entry C vanishes, while for s>1 the second
inequality reduces to (t-1)/(s-1)>=(ct-1)/(cs-1).

Set L=max(A d,D u_s). Direct multiplication in(3) gives new entries

    A_0=A L,                F_0=F D u_s,
    D_0=max(A D d,D^2 u_s,A F u_f),
    C_0=max(A F u_s,C^2 u_f),
    E_(I+2)=[[A_0,D_0],[C_0,F_0]].                     (12)

These entries satisfy, without any division by a potentially zero entry,

    A_0 F_0<=C_0 D_0,  C_0 u_f<=D_0 u_s.             (13)

For the first inequality use D_0>=D L and C_0>=A F u_s. For the
second, the two possibilities in C_0 are bounded by D_0 u_s/u_f:
D_0>=A F u_f treats the first, and D_0>=D^2 u_s together with(11)
treats the second.

For every j>=I+2 both rays must cross at checkpoint j, even at the
smallest permitted secants. Write u_s(j)=b_j M_slow-1 and
u_f(j)=b_j M_fast-1. Their ratio u_f(j)/u_s(j) decreases with j.
Suppose E_j=[[A_j,D_j],[C_j,F_j]] satisfies

    A_j F_j<=C_j D_j, C_j u_f(j)<=D_j u_s(j), D_j>0.

Multiplication by the pure crossing B_j yields exactly

    D_(j+1)=D_j^2 u_s(j),
    A_(j+1)=A_j D_j u_s(j),
    F_(j+1)=F_j D_j u_s(j),
    C_(j+1)=max(A_j F_j u_s(j),C_j^2 u_f(j)).           (14)

The first product inequality persists because the term A_j F_j u_s(j)
occurs in C_(j+1). The ratios A_j/D_j and F_j/D_j are constant, while
C_(j+1)/D_(j+1)<=C_j/D_j. Together with decreasing u_f/u_s, this proves
the second product inequality at the next level. Equation(13) starts
the induction. The full two-state envelope is therefore controlled by
the scalar recurrence for D_j in(14).

For clarity its starting value in(12) is the maximum of three terms:

    T_1=p_s^2 p_f^2 (t-1)^2(cs-1),
    T_2=p_s^3 p_f (1-s/c)(t-1)(1-s)_+,
    T_3=p_s^2 p_f^2 (1-s/c)(1-t/c)(ct-1).              (15)

The positivity D_0>0 and a uniform ratio bound follow as follows. Every
entry in(12) is less than16. If t<=2, T_3>=(1/27^4)(72/845)>
1/(12*27^4). If t>=2 and s<=3/4, T_2>=(1/27^4)(37/208) exceeds that
same bound. If t>=2 and s>=3/4, T_1>=(1/27^4)(19/20) does so. Thus

    max(A_0,D_0,C_0,F_0)/D_0 <= K_sc=192*27^4.         (16)

By(14) this ratio bound holds at every later level.

## 6. The off-diagonal recurrence decays uniformly

We prove, writing J=m-I-2>=0,

    D_m <= exp[-2^J/400].                             (17)

Here D_(I+2)=D_0 in the notation of(12). To avoid ambiguity, recurrence(14)
propagates each of the three starting terms in(15) independently; their
maximum is the propagated D_0 because positive powers commute with max.

The future factors are c^ell s-1 with ell=2,3,..., and are all greater
than1. For any starting term T, their complete infinite logarithmic
budget, divided by 2^J, is bounded above by

    log T + log(c^2)+(1/2)log[s(c^2 s-1)].              (18)

To derive(18), index future factors by k=1,2,..., so they equal
c^(k+1)s-1. Keep the first correction log(1-1/(c^2 s)) exactly with
weight1/2 and drop the later negative corrections. The sums of 2^(-k)
and k2^(-k) are1 and2, giving(18). Extending a finite tail is valid
because all added original logarithms are positive.

### Case 1/c<s<=1

In this range p_s contains exactly the low factors of P_sec(I+2,M_slow),
whose base value is

    p_s^4 (1-s/c)^2 q_sec(cs).

From(5),

    p_f(t-1)/[p_s(1-s/c)] <= c/e<1.                    (19)

Indeed the logarithmic expression increases with s and t on s<=1,
t<=c and equals c/e at s=1,t=c. Thus T_1 and T_2 are at most this
base value. At all future levels q_sec(c^ell s)=c^ell s-1. The index k
in(10) is either I or I+1, so their propagated values are at most
exp[-2^J/400].

For T_3 use p_s^2 p_f^2<=exp[-5(s+t)/4]. For fixed s the logarithm
of exp(-5t/4)(1-t/c)(ct-1) decreases for t>=1. Replacing t by1 in
(18) therefore gives the upper bound

    F_low(s)=-5(s+1)/4+log[(c-s)(c-1)^2]
                         +(1/2)log[s(c^2 s-1)].        (20)

On [1/c,1], F_low''<-1: the last two negative curvature terms alone
have magnitudes greater than1/2+1/2. At s=2/3,

    F_low'(2/3)=-407/7627,
    (c-s)^2(c-1)^4 s(c^2s-1)
       <(163/60)^4(13/12)<exp(49/12).

The last upper comparison uses e>163/60 and exp(1/12)>13/12. Thus
F_low(2/3)<-1/24. Since (407/7627)^2/2<1/600, its tangent parabola
proves F_low(s)<-1/25 throughout the interval. This is stronger than
the exponent required by(17).

### Case 1<=s<=t<=c

T_2=0. First, the function p(t)^2(1-t/c)(ct-1) is decreasing for
t>=1. The derivative of 2log p is at most-5/4, and the remaining
logarithmic derivative is -1/(c-t)+c/(ct-1). Therefore

    T_3<=p_s^4(1-s/c)^2(cs-1)
         <=P_sec(I+2,M_slow).

The index k in(10) is I-1 or I, so the same propagation proves(17)
for this term.

For T_1 use the quadratic improvement in(6). Its bound in(18) is

    F_high(s,t)=-5(s+t)/4-(s^2+t^2)/35+2log(t-1)
                  +log(cs-1)+log(c^2)
                  +(1/2)log[s(c^2s-1)].               (21)

The part depending on t has second derivative at most-25/32. Its
derivative at t=c is-26/175. Completing the tangent parabola gives

    F_high(s,t)<=F_high(s,c)+1/60.                     (22)

The function s->F_high(s,c) has second derivative less than-2/5.
For example the four positive magnitudes have sum bounded below by

    2/35+c^2/(c^2-1)^2+1/(2c^2)
                              +c^4/(2(c^3-1)^2)>2/5.

At s=7/4 its derivative is -213097/10765020, with magnitude less
than1/40. The argument of its combined logarithm is

    (c-1)^2 c^2 (cs-1)*sqrt[s(c^2s-1)] <268<exp(28/5).

The first inequality follows on squaring rational quantities. The
second follows from
(163/60)^5[1+3/5+(3/5)^2/2+(3/5)^3/6]>268.
Its tangent-parabola correction is less than1/1000. Hence(22) is
strictly less than

    28/5-87/16-3929/14000+1/60+1/1000
       =-211/2100<-1/10.

This proves(17) for T_1 and completes all cases. Every displayed
rational comparison is a finite arithmetic inequality; none asserts
a numerical scan or an optimized trajectory.

## 7. Radius, complete objective area, and early horizons

In the overlapping case equations(16)--(17) give

    |x_(Nsc_m)|<=K_sc exp[-2^(m-I-2)/400]|x_0|,
                                                    m>=I+2. (23)

First suppose the bands hold everywhere on their rays. Then integration
from the minimizer gives f(x)<=M_sigma x^2/2. In(23) use M_sigma<=M_fast
and M_fast=t/(a c^I)<=c/(a c^I). With J=m-I-2, the normalized error is
at most

    (c^3 K_sc^2/a)c^(-m)c^J exp(-2^J/200)
       <=(40000 c^3 K_sc^2/a)c^(-m).                   (24)

For m<=I the signs cannot change, all envelope factors are at most1,
and M_fast<=(c/a)c^(-m). For m=I+1 every entry of E_(I+1) is at most
c-1, giving the bound c^2(c-1)^2 c^(-m)/a. These are smaller than(24).
The separated case was bounded in section4 by(7), including its own
two early ranges by the same estimates. Thus the global-band assertion
holds at every horizon.

For the stated queried-only hypotheses take m>=1. The final step of
Hsc_m is a<1/2. Smoothness and g(0)=0 ensure it preserves any nonzero
ray. Write x_pre for the last query and x_end for the terminal point.
If a query or iterate already has zero gradient, the remaining objective
is zero. Otherwise monotonicity and(1) give, on its ray sigma,

    |g(x_end)|<=|g(x_pre)|<=M_sigma |x_pre|
                    <=M_sigma |x_end|/(1-a).

The entire area between0 and x_end obeys

    f(x_end)=integral_0^|x_end| |g(sigma r)| dr
                <=|x_end| |g(x_end)|.

Thus the preceding endpoint estimate with M_sigma x_end^2 is enlarged
by at most2/(1-a)<4. Formula(2), with the chosen C_sc, follows. This
retains all function-value area, even if the derivative is arbitrary
between or outside the queries. At m=0, 1-smoothness directly gives
2f(x_0)<=x_0^2, also smaller than(2).

## 8. Exact benchmark comparison and scope

The accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d defines the exact benchmark. Write
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 and B_n=2S_bullet(n)+1. Its shifted recursions are

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(C_j,C_(n-j-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gamma(C_j,B_(n-j-1)),
    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    Gamma(A,B)=2A+B/2+sqrt(B^2+8AB)/2.

Here is an elementary stronger growth estimate, proved rather than
imported from an unverified finding. Homogeneity and the defining
quadratic of Omega show that
Omega(A,B)<=(A^(3/4)+B^(3/4))^(4/3) is equivalent to
t^(4/3)+(1-t)^(4/3)+[t(1-t)]^(4/3)<=1 on[0,1]. Put u=t^(1/3),
v=(1-t)^(1/3). The difference from1 equals

    u^3 v^3[1/(1+u+u^2)+1/(1+v+v^2)-uv]>=0,

because each reciprocal is at least1/3 and uv<=2^(-2/3)<2/3.
Induction gives C_n<=(n+1)^(4/3). Also Gamma(A,B)<=B+4A by squaring
the square-root comparison, and induction using
u^(4/3)+v^(4/3)<=(u+v)^(4/3) gives B_n<=1+4n^(4/3).

Consequently(2), divided by U_(Nsc_m)^ZJ=1/B_(Nsc_m), is at most

    C_sc c_sc^(-m)[1+4(2^m-1)^(4/3)] ->0,

since c_sc^3=2197/125>16. All constants are uniform over the function,
the two secant scales, their relative phase, and horizon-dependent knots.

The newly closed gap is arbitrary relative phase of two bounded secant
bands. The proof makes no claim when a single ray's queried secants
span more than13/5. The independent factor-four relaxation already
has divergent illegal trajectories; dropping (1) requires new fixed-profile
information, not merely rerunning the matrix recursion. No fact graph
node has been created by this document.

<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-000817 -->
# Two static scalar spatial laws on the original near-critical word

Status: self-contained analytical draft, UNVERIFIED, no fact_id. Worker max,
allocated round56 after completed55; completed ceiling56/attempted57. No
mathematical numerical evaluation, solver, symbolic program, formal request,
installation, consult, or subagent was used. This document does not establish
the original target. Its two negative screens are actual error upper bounds,
not an interpretation of weak lower certificates.

Controlling assignment: TASK.md; initial master guidance deebd8471c9ff5a7.
Later guidance f8408daf213a461d leaves this bounded analytical task unchanged
and closes every remaining formal grant. Plan finding: 6ec9e4bb4db97c3b.

## 1. Scope recovered before choosing the laws

The following are shared, UNVERIFIED drafts, not premises of the proofs below.

* 1a23bf9f1a7799fd uses c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), and the word
  Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,a_sc*c_sc^j,Hsc_j), without a +1 bridge. It treats a
  specified log-periodic derivative made of unit ramps and plateaus. Its
  extension covers every COMMON plateau parameter ell>=1 on the two rays,
  arbitrary independent ray phases, every initial point, and all crossings.
  Its unequal-ell statement retains the stated calibrated range
  b_r<=ell_sigma<=1+(S_r-log c_sc)/(c_sc-1), m>=r>=17. Neither statement
  covers arbitrary nonperiodic scalar derivatives or the original word here.
* 3d2994746c1d983e uses that same c_sc,a_sc and no-+1 word. It allows
  arbitrary horizon-dependent functions and knots, but every nonzero queried
  secant on EACH ray must fit that ray's fixed band
  M_sigma/c_sc<=|g(x)|/|x|<=M_sigma, with 0<M_sigma<=1. The two ray scales
  and phases are unrestricted relative to one another. Its conclusion is
  still conditional on those per-ray factor13/5 bands.
* 4d3911499920adbe uses that same no-+1 word. Its channels are precisely
  accepted F_ray, all scalar quadratics, its explicitly defined single-cap
  quadratic-prefix/affine-suffix channel, and the continuous two-curvature
  homogeneous family f(x)=u*x^2/2 on x>=0 and v*x^2/2 on x<=0. Its direct
  two-curvature proof is for u,v in (0,1], with zero curvatures addressed by
  continuity if included. It is not a bound for derivatives with additional
  spatial slope changes. Its horizon bounds start at m>=18.
* e08ee0674ce368d3 DOES use the original word, for m>=128. It fixes profiles
  g(sigma*R*t)=sigma*mu*R*G_sigma(t), with G_sigma(t)=t on [0,1],
  0<=t*G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow*G_sigma(t) outside, and a finite mutual ray ratio
  kappa_ray. Its first sector has mu=1/d_r for an integer r>=0,
  1/2<=p_grow<=3/4 and kappa_ray*(p_grow/(1-p_grow))^p_grow<=23/10.
  Its second has 0<mu<=1/d_2, 1/2<=p_grow<=2/3 and the corresponding product
  <=7/4. These are genuine restrictions, not a complete scalar reduction.

Accepted scopes were also checked. Fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a constructs its
specified outward alternating-ray stages, including offsets and retained
areas; it does not exhaust scalar trajectories. Fact b98bb807d514e80d gives
K_n and the original benchmark recurrences, not an upper bound on all Huber
trajectories or all scalar functions. Fact 1252b8823bb5a290 supplies complete
directed interpolation and its stated unqueried-terminal reduction, with no
scalar losslessness claim. Fact 62a7d9329121fd75 compares its finite triangle
only to scalar quadratics, the complete specified ray channel, and the full
specified complex channel, NOT to all nonlinear scalar functions.

Only fact b98bb807d514e80d is needed as a mathematical predecessor below,
for the definitions and accepted bounds on the ORIGINAL benchmark. Every
new scalar calculation is reproduced in this joint draft. In particular no
shared unverified closure is imported as a lemma.

## 2. Original word, exact prefix products, and benchmark

Fix an integer m>=32 and retain this SAME m in every child. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), t=1/m, beta=1-t,
    c_m=lambda*(1+t), a_m=beta/c_m,
    c=c_m, a=a_m (local aliases), b_j=a*c^j, d_j=1+b_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), j=0,...,m-1,
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.

The letter b_j in this document is the bridge EXCESS, not a physical step.
Let ell(i)=nu_2(i+1), where nu_2(k) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive
integer k. The actual physical step at index i is h_i=d_(ell(i)). Let s_2(i)
be the number of ones in the binary expansion of i, including s_2(0)=0.
For 0<=i<=n define

    Prod_i=product_(0<=r<i)(h_r-1)=beta^i*c^(-s_2(i)).              (2.1)

Here and below all finite sums and products are mathematical definitions;
none was evaluated by a program. To prove (2.1), binary carrying gives
s_2(i+1)=s_2(i)+1-nu_2(i+1). The ratio of its right sides at i+1 and i
is beta*c^(ell(i)-1)=a*c^ell(i)=h_i-1. Both sides start at1. In particular

    Prod_n=beta^n*c^(-m).                                        (2.2)

The S_j recurrence S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j gives

    S_j+1=A*c^j+(1-A)*2^j, A=a/(c-2)=beta/[c*(c-2)].          (2.3)

Since c>lambda>2 and lambda*(lambda-2)=1, we have 0<A<1, and hence
S_m+1<=c^m. These parameters also have 0<a<1, 0<beta<1, and c<3:
lambda<5/2 and c<=(5/2)*(33/32)=165/64<3.

Write C_k=1+S_circle(k), B_k=1+2*S_bullet(k), U_k^ZJ=1/B_k.
Fact b98bb807d514e80d supplies

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_k=max_(0<=r<k) Omega(C_r,C_(k-r-1)),
    B_k=max_(0<=r<k) G(C_r,B_(k-r-1)),
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4*u*v))/2,
    G(u,v)=2*u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8*u*v)/2,
    B_k<=1+4*k^(log_2(3)).

Thus, on the original physical length n=2^m-1,

    B_n<=5*3^m.                                              (2.4)

No asymptotic constant or numerically evaluated benchmark is needed here.
All payoffs use L=1 and TRUE distance to the minimizer set:

    V=2*(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.

Multiplying f by an arbitrary L>0 and using steps h_i/L preserves all
positions and this normalized payoff.

## 3. Law I: a flat minimizer interval with two unit-curvature sides

For any width w>=0 let I_w=[-w,0] and define the single static function

              (x+w)^2/2, x<-w,
    f_w(x)=   0,         -w<=x<=0,
              x^2/2,    x>0.

Let p_w(x)=min(0,max(-w,x)) be the interval projection, and put
g_w(x)=x-p_w(x). The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes
one, zero, one in spatial order. Consequently f_w is convex and1-smooth,
with exactly the minimizer set I_w and minimum0. For w>0 it is genuinely
nonlinear. We always normalize using distance to I_w, not distance to its
midpoint or another more distant chosen minimizer.

### 3.1 All directed inequalities and all integrated values

At every point, with y=p_w(x), we have

    x=g+y, f_w(x)=g^2/2, y in I_w.

For any two sampled points i,j, including ANY chosen optimum, the full
signed directed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2
        =g_i*(y_j-y_i)<=0.                                  (3.1)

Indeed g_i>0 implies y_i=0>=y_j; g_i<0 implies y_i=-w<=y_j;
and g_i=0 gives equality. This is an explicit all-pairs proof, including
both directions, all optimum rows, repeated points, and every plateau area.
The formula f=g^2/2 integrates the fixed derivative from the actual interval;
it is not a deletion of a previously accumulated area.

### 3.2 Actual GD, zero steps, capture, and exact revisits

Let Dist_i=dist(x_i,I_w), with Dist_0>0. At any outside point, a step h>=0 gives

    Dist_next=(1-h)*Dist_i,                     0<=h<=1,
    Dist_next=((h-1)*Dist_i-w)_+,               h>1.                (3.2)

For h<=1 the point stays on its current side or lands on its boundary.
For h>1 it either lands inside I_w or crosses to the opposite outside side.
An inside point has zero gradient and stays at precisely that same point.
This proves (3.2) directly on both sides of the interval. A zero step repeats
the complete jet. There is no restart at a different minimizer after capture.

Exact outside revisits are also legal: for any w>0,D>0, two consecutive
steps h=2+w/D take D to -w-D and then back to D. Each outside value is D^2/2.
This illustration uses the SAME law, not an additional candidate family.
Its only purpose is to make the exact-revisit convention explicit.

On our original word all h_i>1. Define Ret_0=0 and

    Ret_(i+1)=(h_i-1)*Ret_i+1,
    Ret_i=Prod_i*sum_(r=1)^i 1/Prod_r.                              (3.3)

Repeated application of (3.2) gives the exact formula

    Dist_i=(Prod_i*Dist_0-w*Ret_i)_+.                                 (3.4)

For the induction, ((b*z)_+-w)_+=(b*z-w)_+ when b,w>=0;
applying this to the preceding affine expression proves the next step.
If capture has occurred, the expression remains zero as required.

Consequently, for ALL w>=0 and every initial point outside I_w,

    V_w=(Dist_n/Dist_0)^2<=Prod_n^2=beta^(2*n)*c^(-2*m).             (3.5)

This is an upper bound on the actual error of the entire specified law,
including horizon-dependent widths, either initial side, and arbitrary
initial distances. It is not an upper bound on arbitrary scalar functions.

### 3.3 A concrete nonlinear, noncaptured trajectory and its full spatial order

Here is one exact choice with w>0 and every queried distance positive.
The Prod_i in (2.1) are pairwise distinct. To see this, all are in Q(sqrt(2)).
The nontrivial conjugation fixes beta and sends c to
c'=(1-sqrt(2))*(1+1/m). If beta^(i-j)*c^(-s_2(i)+s_2(j))=1,
divide by its conjugate. Since |c/c'|=lambda^2>1, the integer exponent
of c must be zero. Then beta^(i-j)=1 implies i=j.

Define the positive finite quantities

    Delta_sp=min_(0<=i<j<=n)|Prod_i-Prod_j|,
    Ret_max=max_(0<=i<=n) Ret_i,
    w=min{Prod_n/(2*Ret_n), Delta_sp/(4*Ret_max)}.                    (3.6)

These finite expressions define the width before the run; they are not a
numerical search or an unproved optimization problem. Start at x_0=1 and
choose the closest minimizer0, so the TRUE initial squared distance is1.
Since Ret_i/Prod_i is increasing in i, (3.6) implies

    Dist_i=Prod_i-w*Ret_i>=Prod_i/2>0,
    x_i=Dist_i if i is even, and x_i=-w-Dist_i if i is odd.         (3.7)

Every step thus crosses the whole minimizer interval. Equations (3.2)--(3.7)
give every physical point, derivative, value, and GD update without deleting
any interval or knot.

For completeness the spatial order, which is not chronological, is exact.
Put alpha=-log(beta)/log(c)>0 and order_i=s_2(i)+alpha*i. Then
Prod_i=c^(-order_i), and the order_i are distinct by the preceding argument.
For every pair, |w*(Ret_i-Ret_j)|<=Delta_sp/4<|Prod_i-Prod_j|, so Dist_i-Dist_j has exactly
the sign of Prod_i-Prod_j. Every odd-indexed point is less than -w; every
even-indexed point is positive. Among even indices, increasing order_i means
decreasing x; among odd indices, increasing order_i means increasing x.
This completely specifies the total order by explicit, distinct real keys
for each m, with NO guessed phase comparisons and no ties. The fixed
minimizer interval lies between the two groups. Other widths may have ties;
(3.1)--(3.4) still apply without changing any previously defined value.

The terminal point is -w-Dist_n, since n is odd, and

    f_w(x_n)=(Prod_n-w*Ret_n)^2/2,
    Prod_n^2/4<=V_w=(Prod_n-w*Ret_n)^2<Prod_n^2.                     (3.8)

Thus this nonlinear example has a proved exact payoff, not just a lower
certificate. Nevertheless (2.4)--(3.5) imply uniformly for the whole law

    B_n*V_w<=5*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0.                         (3.9)

The flat-minimum mechanism is weak on the original word even when its
width is chosen from the entire word and every queried point remains outside.

## 4. Law II: a one-sided Huber shelf with an actual inward return

Fix ONE function, independent of m:

              x^2/2, x<=1,
    F_sh(x)=  x-1/2, x>=1,
    g_sh(x)=min(x,1).

It is convex, differentiable, globally1-smooth, with unique minimizer0 and
minimum0. Its negative ray is quadratic and its positive ray eventually
has constant gradient. In particular the two outer ray gradients have no
finite multiplicative comparison bound of the type in e08ee0674ce368d3.
The complete function is fixed throughout the trajectory.

Define y(x)=(x-1)_+. Then at every x

    x=g+y, F_sh(x)=g^2/2+y, g<=1, y>=0, (1-g)*y=0.          (4.1)

The positive proximal value y is retained on every return to the shelf.
For ANY two points, including optimum0 and repeated queries,

    Q_ij=(1-g_i)*(y_i-y_j)=-(1-g_i)*y_j<=0.                (4.2)

This explicit factorization proves ALL directed inequalities for the entire
trajectory, including every incoming and outgoing terminal row. It uses
the integrated value in (4.1), not just the slope or a pointwise envelope.

### 4.1 Parameters of the selected return branch

Use exactly the enclosing-m d_0,d_1,d_2,d_3 from Section2 and put

    w_sh=beta^2*c,
    v_sh=w_sh-1-beta,
    R_sh=S_3+1=8+4*a+2*beta+beta*c.

For every m>=32 the following strict inequalities hold:

    0<v_sh<a*beta,
    c*v_sh<beta^2<1,
    1+beta<w_sh<2+a.                                     (4.3)

Here is an exact proof without numerical evaluation. Since beta>=31/32
and c>12/5, w_sh>(31/32)^2*(12/5)>2, the last inequality following from
12*31^2>10*32^2. Thus v_sh>0. Also

    c*v_sh-beta^2
      =lambda^2*(1-t^2)^2-lambda*(2+t-t^2)-(1-2*t+t^2)
      <=(2-lambda)*t-(lambda+2)*t^2<0.

The inequality uses (1-t^2)^2<=1-t^2 and lambda^2=2*lambda+1.
It proves c*v_sh<beta^2 and hence v_sh<a*beta. Finally

    c*(w_sh-2-a)
      =beta^2*c^2-2*c-beta
      <=lambda^2-2*lambda*(1+t)-(1-t)
      =-(2*lambda-1)*t<0.

Choose any delta in the FULL interval [0,v_sh/a] and start at

    X_0=R_sh+delta, z_sh=v_sh-a*delta>=0.                  (4.4)

The squared distance to the unique minimizer is exactly (R_sh+delta)^2.
The endpoint delta=v_sh/a is permitted and will give a genuine zero landing.

### 4.2 First ten physical steps, with the old shelf intact

The first seven steps are the actual word H_3, with levels

    0,1,0,2,0,1,0.

For 0<=i<=7 let Prefix_sh(i) be the sum of its first i physical steps. Then

    X_i=R_sh+delta-Prefix_sh(i)>=1+delta,
    g_i=1, F_sh(X_i)=X_i-1/2.                             (4.5)

All seven updates are therefore exactly the original GD updates, and
X_7=1+delta. The next three original physical levels are3,0,1, giving

    X_8=delta-b_3,      g_8=X_8,   F_sh(X_8)=X_8^2/2,
    X_9=w_sh-a*delta,   g_9=1,     F_sh(X_9)=X_9-1/2,
    X_10=z_sh,          g_10=z_sh, F_sh(X_10)=z_sh^2/2.      (4.6)

The equations follow in order from
X_8=X_7-d_3, X_9=-a*X_8, and X_10=X_9-d_1.
Their region assumptions are proved, not guessed:

    b_3-delta>=(w_sh-v_sh)/a=(1+beta)/a>1,
    X_9=1+beta+z_sh>1,
    0<=z_sh<=v_sh<1.

Furthermore delta<=v_sh/a<beta implies

    X_7<X_9<X_6, with X_6=2+a+delta.                      (4.7)

The right inequality follows from X_9<=w_sh<2+a<=X_6.
Thus X_9 is a strict inward return into the SAME spatial interval [X_7,X_6]
traversed during the last step of the earlier H_3. The shelf gradient there
is1 and its retained proximal value is y_9=X_9-1>0. Nothing is redefined on
this return. This is not an outward-only fresh-ray construction.

### 4.3 Every remaining query, spatial order, and ties

For EVERY actual physical index 10<=i<=n the exact formula is

    X_i=(-1)^(i-10)*z_sh*beta^(i-10)*c^(2-s_2(i)).          (4.8)

If z_sh=0 this means precisely that the actual point0 is repeated forever.
Assume z_sh>0. Since s_2(i)>=1 for i>=10,

    |X_i|<=c*z_sh<=c*v_sh<beta^2<1.                       (4.9)

Thus every one of these queries really has gradient X_i and value X_i^2/2.
At i=10, s_2(10)=2 gives the correct point z_sh. Binary carrying then gives

    X_(i+1)/X_i=-beta*c^(ell(i)-1)=-(h_i-1)=1-h_i.

This verifies every remaining GD update on the original physical word.
No child clock was restarted. In particular the bound (4.9) certifies every
intermediate query, not merely the endpoints of large blocks. Gradients
continue to vary inside those blocks.

Here is the complete spatial order. X_8<-1 is below every later negative
point. Every positive point of the tail is less than1. The old positive
queries are ordered

    1<=X_7<X_9<X_6<X_5<...<X_0.

The optimum0 separates the positive and negative tail points. For i>=10,
even i means positive and odd i means negative. Within either sign, their
magnitudes are proportional to c^(-order_i), with the SAME explicit keys
order_i=s_2(i)+alpha*i used in Section3.3. Thus increasing order_i gives
decreasing positive coordinates and increasing negative coordinates.
There are no ties among those keys: an equality of two magnitudes would
give beta^(i-j)*c^(-s_2(i)+s_2(j))=1, already proved to force i=j.
There are also no ties with the older groups by their strict separating
intervals. When z_sh=0, all indices i>=10 instead coincide with optimum0,
with gradient and value0. These are all ties in this construction.

The word itself has no zero steps. If a zero step is inserted, its actual
map x->x-0*g_sh(x) is the identity and repeats its jet; (4.2) remains valid.
Formula (4.8) refers to the original positive steps and can be pulled back
along their index injection. No claim transfers B_n to a new padded length.

### 4.4 Exact payoff and a uniform upper screen for this branch

The original n is odd and s_2(n)=m. Therefore

    X_n=-z_sh*beta^(n-10)*c^(2-m),
    F_sh(X_n)=z_sh^2*beta^(2*(n-10))*c^(4-2*m)/2,
    V_sh(delta)=((v_sh-a*delta)/(R_sh+delta))^2
                 *beta^(2*(n-10))*c^(4-2*m).              (4.10)

Over the ENTIRE selected interval 0<=delta<=v_sh/a, the numerator of the
fraction decreases to zero while its positive denominator increases. Its
maximum is attained at delta=0 and equals the displayed expression with
v_sh/R_sh. In particular, using c*v_sh<1, c<3, R_sh>1, and beta<1,

    V_sh(delta)<9*c^(-2*m),
    B_n*V_sh(delta)<45*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0.                 (4.11)

These are actual error upper bounds on a fully specified returning family.
For all m>=32 they already give V_sh<U_n^ZJ: lambda^2>9/2 implies
3/lambda^2<2/3, and 45*(2/3)^10=46080/59049<1. The same comparison for
Law I follows from (3.9). No guessed decimal or numerical phase is involved.

## 5. Counterchecks preventing an overstatement or a reset

### 5.1 Erasing the return's old area violates a directed inequality

On the trajectory (4.4)--(4.8), replace ONLY the value at X_9 by the falsely
reset core value g_9^2/2=1/2, leaving its position and derivative unchanged.
The earlier point X_7 has gradient1 and value X_7-1/2. Its row to point9 is

    Q_(7,9)=(X_7-1/2)-1/2+(X_9-X_7)=X_9-1>0.

This is an exact illegal directed pair, not an appeal to intuition about
history. The correct value X_9-1/2 has retained area X_9-1 and satisfies
every row by (4.2). The construction therefore directly tests the nonzero
proximal-height issue in the assignment.

### 5.2 The branch bound is FALSE for all starts of the same shelf function

Keep exactly F_sh and the same original H_m, but start at

    X_0^Hub=2*S_m+1.

Every actual query stays in the positive shelf, since even the terminal
point is S_m+1>1. Thus all gradients are1, the terminal value is S_m+1/2,
the true initial radius is 2*S_m+1, and

    V_Hub=1/(2*S_m+1).                                   (5.1)

This is the already familiar canonical Huber mechanism, not a third law
or a new claim of useful profile improvement. It prevents extrapolating
(4.11) to the full one-sided Huber family.

One can see its contrast at the ORIGINAL B scale without an external
asymptotic constant. The accepted recurrences imply inductively
C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j, by choosing equal children and Omega(u,u)=lambda*u.
They also imply B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j: choose equal child lengths, use
monotonicity of G (each displayed term increases in either nonnegative
argument), and G(lambda^(j-1),lambda^(j-1))=4*lambda^(j-1)
>=lambda^j. The base j=0 is1. From (2.3),

    B_n*V_Hub>lambda^m/(2*c^m)
              =1/[2*(1+1/m)^m]>1/6.                     (5.2)

For the last bound, the binomial theorem gives
(1+1/m)^m<=sum_(k=0)^m 1/k!<3: for k>=2, k!>=2^(k-1), and the resulting
geometric upper bound has sum1, with a strict inequality by k=3.
Thus a vanishing B-scaled bound for ALL starts of this same function would
be contradicted by (5.1)--(5.2). No such broader bound is asserted here.

## 6. Achieved scope and the missing interface

There are exactly two selected derivative laws. Law I has an actual uniform
upper bound for its entire interval-distance family, plus a fully defined
nonlinear example with exact ordering and a two-sided terminal estimate.
Law II has an exact returning trajectory for every m>=32 and every delta in
its specified interval, a full spatial-order description, nonzero retained
proximal heights, all directed inequalities, genuine within-block variation,
and an exact terminal payoff. Its whole selected returning branch is weak.
The zero-landing endpoint and inserted zero-step convention preserve all jets.

Neither diagnostic supplies a new witness with B_n*V approaching1. Even a
successful diagnostic on all these H_m would leave the every-n/every-h
target as a separate obligation. The present result is narrower: the two
specified screens do NOT exclude all nonlinear scalar functions, do NOT
exclude other initial points of F_sh, and do NOT bound unrestricted W_n.
No proposed bang-bang, monotonicity, symmetry, or spatial-order reduction
has been declared lossless. The old closed sectors are not enlarged by name.

The surviving interface is to construct a static scalar derivative with
additional retained spatial information (or another initialization on a
candidate function) whose ACTUAL original-word payoff is large enough at
B_n scale, with the same complete directed and true-distance audit. A list
of feasible interpolation variables alone would not resolve this interface.
All new mathematics in this document remains UNVERIFIED and cannot be used
as a fact predecessor until a separately authorized verification succeeds.

## 7. Retrieval and resource record

The query-memory skill recovered the named drafts, recent dead ends, and
the accepted statements. The direct-proving and construct-counterexamples
procedures were used for the two joint screens and the two counterchecks.
Matlas was queried for scalar spatial interpolation and then for interval
distance/one-sided Huber functions. The returned statements did not supply
the needed mechanism; none is used as a theorem. A primary-source web search
returned the Silver schedule paper and long-step background, but supplied
no required lemma. No external result is a proof premise here, so there are
no new external_refs. An additional paper download was not needed for these
two laws: both reduce to the already audited projection/interpolation and
Huber setting, and all formulas and pair inequalities are proved explicitly.

Initial broad memory output was truncated. The assignment, latest guidance,
four precise scope passages, and actually used benchmark proof were reread

# Critical ruler: fixed asymmetric derivatives with within-block variation

Status: complete mathematical CANDIDATE, not verifier-accepted. No numerical
solver or fact_submit is allocated to this round. All new ingredients are
proved together below. Earlier C23/C24/C26 concern a different ruler and are
motivation, not premises. The only accepted input is the exact benchmark
recurrence in fact b98bb807d514e80d. This does not upper-bound unrestricted W.

## 1. The actual schedule and two whole-family statements

Fix the actual horizon parameter m>=128. Hold

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, K=1/(2S_m+1)

fixed throughout the run. Set r_s=c-2 and A_mass=a/r_s. Direct summation gives

    S_j+1=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                 (1)

For a number p_grow in[1/2,1), put beta_grow=1-p_grow and

    C_grow=(p_grow/beta_grow)^p_grow.

Choose two locally absolutely continuous profiles G_+,G_- on[0,infinity),
equal to t on[0,1], with

    0<=t G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow G_sigma(t) a.e. for t>1,
    1/kappa_ray<=G_+(t)/G_-(t)<=kappa_ray for t>=1.    (2)

Here kappa_ray>=1 and sigma denotes either sign. For any core radius R>0
and curvature mu in(0,1], define ONE FIXED derivative and its objective by

    g(sigma R t)=sigma mu R G_sigma(t),
    f(x)=integral_0^x g(s) ds.                       (3)

All parameters and the entire profiles may depend on m; they cannot change
during GD. These classes allow unbounded radial secant variation, asymmetry,
and arbitrarily many nonperiodic knots. Ordinary ruler subblocks need not
have constant gradients. Initial points are arbitrary and are not saturated.

Statement A, reciprocal cores. Suppose

    1/2<=p_grow<=3/4, kappa_ray C_grow<=23/10,
    mu=1/d_r for ANY integer r>=0.                  (4)

Every trajectory on H_m which strictly crosses zero hits zero subsequently.
The exact supremum, over the profiles, radius and nonzero initial point, is

    sup 2f(x_n)/x_0^2=1/(2S_m+d_r).                 (5)

This is attained by a symmetric Huber derivative. For r>=m there is no
strict crossing, and the same conclusion holds. In particular the maximum
over r in this class is 1/(2S_m+d_0)<K.

Statement B, arbitrary small curvatures. Suppose instead

    1/2<=p_grow<=2/3, kappa_ray C_grow<=7/4,
    0<mu<=1/d_2.                                   (6)

No resonance or reciprocal-curvature condition is imposed. Every trajectory
obeys

    2f(x_n)/x_0^2<=K.                              (7)

The supremum over this entire class lies between 1/(2S_m+d_2) and K.
Since d_2 is bounded uniformly in m, its ratio to K tends to one.

A concrete scalable piecewise-linear subclass in (6) can be fixed before
any query as follows. Put T=4^m. On each ray independently partition[1,T]
by arbitrary knots whose successive ratios are at most4, and interpolate
the values sqrt(t) linearly. Beyond T keep the value sqrt(T) constant;
inside[0,1] use t. The two partitions may differ and may have any number
of additional horizon-dependent knots. For example, one partition has
knots4^k, and the other has1,2,8,...,2*4^(m-1),4^m. Any segment with
endpoint ratio q<=4 has maximal elasticity sqrt(q)/(1+sqrt(q))<=2/3.
On[1,T] its chord is between(2sqrt(2)/3)sqrt(t) and sqrt(t): the minimum
chord-to-square-root ratio is2q^(1/4)/(1+sqrt(q)), minimized at q=4.
Thus the two complete profiles are comparable with
kappa_ray=3/(2sqrt(2)), including their common outer plateau. Their
product kappa_ray C_grow=3/2^(5/6)<7/4, verified by raising to the
sixth power:729*128<117649. Taking, for instance, mu=1/(2d_2) and R=1
gives explicit fixed functions with checked spatial order, slopes and
trapezoidal areas. The theorem covers every initial point for these
functions, rather than choosing derivative pieces afresh at queries.

For BOTH classes the exact benchmark from b98bb807d514e80d satisfies

    limsup B_(2^m-1) sup 2f(x_n)/x_0^2<=2/e<1.       (8)

Thus these specified whole scalar mechanisms cannot reach the required
benchmark on the near-critical diagnostic. This is not an all-scalar bound.

## 2. Legality, areas, and uniform schedule estimates

Integrating (2) gives 1<=G_sigma(t)<=t^p_grow<=t for t>=1. Also
0<=G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow t^(p_grow-1)<=1. The unit core and continuity at1
therefore make each profile nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz. Across the origin,
G_+(t)+G_-(u)<=t+u proves the same bound. Hence (3) is nondecreasing,
mu-Lipschitz, and continuous. Its integral is convex, differentiable and
1-smooth, with unique minimizer0. Its complete values, including all outer
areas, are

    f(sigma R t)=mu R^2 F_sigma(t),
    F_sigma(t)=t^2/2                           for t<=1,
    F_sigma(t)=1/2+integral_1^t G_sigma(v)dv     for t>=1. (9)

Consequently0 is the actual closest minimizer, and the normalization in (5)
and (7) is precisely the original squared distance x_0^2.

The following rational bounds hold for every m>=128:

    12/5<c<49/20, c<56/23,
    2/5<a<1/lambda<5/12,
    127/128<=ac<1, 0<a<r_s<1/2, 0<A_mass<1.       (10)

For c<56/23, use sqrt(2)<99/70, so
c<(169/70)(129/128)=21801/8960<56/23. The other c bound follows too.
Since a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda], a<1/lambda=lambda-2<r_s. Its lower bound
follows from a>=(127/128)/(49/20)=635/1568>2/5. Equation (1) implies

    S_j+1<=c^j,
    d_(j+1)/d_j=c-(c-1)/d_j<c.                    (11)

We will also need

    S_j>= (7/4)d_j for every j>=3.                (12)

Indeed S_j-(7/4)d_j equals
a(1/r_s-7/4)c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j-11/4, which increases with j.
For j=3, S_3=7+a(4+2c+c^2)>1603/125, whereas
d_3=1+(ac)c^2<2801/400. The inequality
1603/125>(7/4)(2801/400) proves (12).

## 3. A complete crossing-clock inequality

Abbreviate p=p_grow, beta=1-p and C=C_grow. On either ray define

    Clock_sigma(t)=integral_1^t dv/G_sigma(v), t>0.

It is log t in the core. Outside the core put q_t=t/G_sigma(t).
The derivative condition gives q_t'>=beta/G_sigma(t), and hence

    Clock_sigma(t)<=(q_t-1)/beta, t>=1.            (13)

For every h>1 and every strict overshoot z=hG_sigma(t)-t>0,

    Clock_sigma(z)<= C h-1/beta.                  (14)

Here h is a normalized stepsize mu d_j, not the physical stepsize d_j.
We prove all cases, including short crossing steps and core endpoints.

If z<=1, the bound G_sigma(t)<=t^p holds for every t>=0, also inside the
core. Maximizing h t^p-t by differentiation gives

    z<=(beta C h)^(1/beta).

Thus log z<=log(beta C h)/beta<=C h-1/beta.

If z>=1 and t<=1, then h>=2, and monotonicity gives
Clock_sigma(z)<=z-1<=h-2. This is <=C h-1/beta because
C>=1/(2beta). To prove the latter, the function
p log p+beta log beta is increasing for p>=1/2 and equals-log2 at1/2;
therefore beta C=p^p beta^beta>=1/2.

If z,t>=1 and z>=t, monotonicity and (13) yield

    Clock_sigma(z)<=(q_t-1)/beta+(z-t)/G_sigma(t)
                   =h+(1/beta-2)q_t-1/beta
                   <=h/(2beta)-1/beta<=C h-1/beta,

where q_t<=h/2 and p>=1/2 were used.

Finally, if1<=z<=t, integrating (2) between z and t gives
G_sigma(z)>=G_sigma(t)(z/t)^p. Therefore

    z/G_sigma(z)<=q_t^p(h-q_t)^beta
                  <=p^p beta^beta h=beta C h.

The maximum in the last inequality occurs at q_t=p h; this follows by
differentiating p log q+beta log(h-q). Equation (13) proves (14).

Write D=C h-1/beta. If a crossing lands on the other ray, its new clock
is at most kappa_ray D when D>=0: use the pointwise profile comparison
and integrate if z>=1; for z<1 the new clock is negative. If D<0,
(14) forces z<1, and BOTH ray clocks equal log z, so the new clock is
at most D, with no comparison-factor loss. A noncrossing nonzero step
reduces its own clock by at least h, since

    integral_(t-hG_sigma(t))^t dv/G_sigma(v)>=h.

We also have Clock_sigma(t)<=t-1 for all t>0, using G_sigma>=1 outside
the core and log t<=t-1 inside it.

## 4. Reciprocal cores: every crossing is annihilated

Assume (4), and put sigma_clock=kappa_ray C and
tau_clock=kappa_ray/beta. The function C=(p/(1-p))^p increases for
p>=1/2, so C<=3^(3/4)<23/10. In particular the class is nonempty
throughout the asserted p range. Also (10) gives

    sigma_clock<=23/10<1/r_s.

Since p/beta>=1 and p<=1, C<=(p/beta), so

    tau_clock-sigma_clock=kappa_ray(1/beta-C)>=1.  (15)

If a trajectory ever reaches zero, it stays there. Otherwise choose its
last strict crossing. A crossing requires h=mu d_j>1 because G_sigma(t)<=t.
Thus its level j is greater than r. Every occurrence of d_j in the ruler
is immediately followed by a complete H_j. This copy of H_j contains a
last occurrence of d_r, followed by H_r. The physical mass before that
last d_r, measured from the start of this copy of H_j, is

    S_j-S_r-d_r.                                 (16)

Suppose the overshoot radius z is greater than1. Section3 bounds its
target clock by sigma_clock d_j/d_r-tau_clock. Define
D_j=S_j-sigma_clock d_j. By (1),

    D_j=a(1/r_s-sigma_clock)c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j-1-sigma_clock

is increasing. Consequently

    S_j-S_r-d_r-(sigma_clock d_j-tau_clock d_r)
      =D_j-D_r+(tau_clock-sigma_clock-1)d_r>=0.    (17)

There is no subsequent crossing by our choice of the last one. Each
subsequent nonzero step decreases the target clock by its normalized
stepsize. Equations (16)-(17) force the path into the core by the last
d_r in that copy of H_j. If z<=1 it is already in the core; a
noncrossing core step keeps it there. The last d_r then maps x to
x-d_r mu x=0. This proves the annihilation assertion without assuming
that an arbitrary earlier core entry is permanent.

For completeness, a run with no crossing, on either ray, has final
radius v and gradient magnitude u. Monotonicity gives
|x_0|>=v+S_m u. Since the actual derivative is mu-Lipschitz,

    f(x_n)<=u v-u^2/(2mu).

This follows by integrating the bound |g(t)|<=min(mu|t|,u) from0 to
the final point. Therefore

    2f(x_n)<=2|x_0|u-(2S_m+1/mu)u^2
             <=x_0^2/(2S_m+1/mu).                 (18)

For G_+=G_-=min(t,1), start at x_0=R(1+2mu S_m). Every gradient on
the trajectory equals mu R, all iterates remain beyond the core, and
x_n=R(1+mu S_m). Substitution into (9) gives equality in (18).
Together with annihilation this proves (5), including r>=m.

## 5. Nonresonant cores: a uniform actual-map attraction bound

Assume (6), and abbreviate

    b_j=mu d_j, U_j=mu S_j, eta=7/4,
    rho_core=1/8, k_env=1/8, A_env=c/8.

Let I be the first level with t=b_I>1 and put J=I+1. Because mu<=1/d_2,
I>=3. Also1<t<c by (11). If the horizon m<=I, all its normalized
steps are<=1, so no crossing occurs and (18) already proves (7).

First, ANY crossing run on ANY whole H_j ends at radius less than
rho_core in normalized coordinates x/R. Select its last strict crossing,
at some level l>=I>=3, with h=b_l>1. The following full H_l has
normalized mass U_l>=eta h, by (12). Use Section3 with D=C h-1/beta.

If D>=0, the terminal clock is bounded by
(kappa_ray C-eta)h-kappa_ray/beta. This increases with kappa_ray
at fixed h, so under kappa_ray C<=eta it is at most

    -eta/(beta C)<-105/32.

To justify the strict constant, beta C=p^p beta^beta increases on
[1/2,2/3] and is at most4^(1/3)/3<8/15. If D<0, the terminal clock
is at most(C-eta)h-1/beta<=C-eta-1/beta<=-eta-1=-11/4,
using C<=p/beta and C<=eta. Since exp(11/4)>8, both cases give

    |Phi_j(x/R)|<rho_core for every crossing run.  (19)

Here Phi_j is the actual map of the SAME fixed function under H_j.
A noncrossing, nonzero run starting at radius v instead satisfies

    Clock(radius_end)<=v-1-U_j.                  (20)

We now prove the actual-map invariant, for j>=J and0<=q<=rho_core,

    |x/R|<=q b_j implies
    |Phi_j(x/R)|<=min(A_env q,k_env rho_core).     (21)

This controls both possible signs without assuming an odd map or
asserting that a reachable image is a centered interval.

Base j=J. All steps of H_I are at most1, so a core-started run stays
in the quadratic core. Its multiplier P is nonnegative and
P<=exp(-U_I)<=exp(-eta t). The middle step t has radius factor t-1,
and (t-1)P<(c-1)exp(-eta)<1. Thus a core-started whole H_J stays
in the core and has absolute multiplier Q=(t-1)P^2. Furthermore

    b_J Q<=c t(t-1)exp(-2eta t)
           <=3c/(16eta^3)<=3/35<1/8.             (22)

For the middle inequality use exp(2eta t)>(2eta t)^3/6 and
(t-1)/t^2<=1/4. Also rho_core b_J<c^2/8<1. Thus every input in
(21) is a core input, and its endpoint is at most k_env q, which
implies both bounds in (21).

Induction j>J. Write the actual word as H_(j-1),d_(j-1),H_(j-1),
and let v_1 be the radius after its first block.

If c q<=rho_core, the induction hypothesis applies to the first
block because b_j<=c b_(j-1). It gives
v_1<=min(A_env c q,k_env rho_core)<=q, since A_env c=c^2/8<1.
Thus v_1<=rho_core. The middle step uses the quadratic core and
has output radius |1-b_(j-1)|v_1<=b_(j-1)v_1. The second block,
using parameter v_1 in the induction hypothesis, gives
radius_end<=min(A_env q,k_env rho_core).

If c q>rho_core, then v_1<=rho_core as well. A crossing first block
uses (19). A noncrossing first block uses (20) and (12):

    Clock(v_1)<=rho_core b_j-1-U_(j-1)
               <=-(eta/c-rho_core)b_j-1<-3.       (23)

For the last inequality, eta/c>5/7. Also j>=I+2 and d_I>=d_3>6,
so

    b_j>=b_(I+2)>d_(I+2)/d_I
        =c^2-(c^2-1)/d_I
        >(5c^2+1)/6>5,                            (24)

where c^2>lambda^2>29/5. Therefore the right side of (23) is less
than-1-5(5/7-1/8)=-221/56<-3. Since exp3>8, (23) implies the
desired v_1<rho_core. A zero iterate is harmless. The middle step
now has radius at most b_(j-1)rho_core, so the second induction
application gives radius_end<=k_env rho_core. Because q>rho_core/c
and A_env=c k_env, this also is at most A_env q. The induction is
complete. Notice that it explicitly handles partial-block entries and
subsequent exits; first entry is never presumed permanent capture.

## 6. Nonresonant full objective, with original distance retained

No-crossing runs obey (18), and hence have normalized gap<=K.
For a crossing run with m>=J, (19) places the endpoint in the core.
If its initial normalized radius v_0 is at most rho_core b_m,
(21) gives radius_end<=A_env v_0/b_m. If v_0>rho_core b_m,
(19) gives radius_end<rho_core. In either case, using the complete
core area in (9),

    2f(x_n)/x_0^2<=mu/b_m^2.                     (25)

For m>=I+2, (24), a>2/5 and (11) yield

    b_m d_m>5a c^m>2c^m>2S_m+1.

Since mu/b_m^2=1/(b_m d_m), (25) is strictly below K.

It remains to check the base horizon m=J=I+1, where (25) alone
would discard useful information. If v_0<=1, the entire run stays
in the quadratic core by the base argument, so its normalized gap
is mu Q^2<mu/64. If v_0>1 and the run crosses, (19) gives the same
strict bound mu/64. Finally

    mu(2S_m+1)<2mu c^m<2b_m/a<2c^2/a<125/4<64.

Here b_m=b_J<c^2, c<5/2 and a>2/5 were used. Thus mu/64<K.
This proves (7) for all cases. The lower end of the asserted squeeze
is the Huber construction in Section4 with mu=1/d_2. It belongs to
(6), and has normalized payoff1/(2S_m+d_2).

## 7. Exact benchmark comparison

We use ONLY the exact recursions in accepted fact b98bb807d514e80d.
Write C_n=S_circle(n)+1. Their mass joins are

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,
    Gamma(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2,
    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Omega(C_k,C_l),
    B_n=max_(k+l=n-1) Gamma(C_k,B_l).

Put rho=log_2(lambda). We give the needed sharper estimates directly:

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                (26)

Let E(t)=(1+t)^rho-1. With t=exp z, differentiation gives

    d^2/dz^2 log E(exp z)
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/E(t)^2>0.

The strict inequality follows by differentiating (1+t)^rho-1-rho t.
Convexity at z,-z yields E(t)E(1/t)>=E(1)^2=2. For A=t^rho B
and D=(1+t)^rho B this means (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, so

    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.

Induction proves the first assertion of (26). The inequality
Gamma(A,D)<=4A+D follows by squaring sqrt(D^2+8AD)<=D+4A.
Induction using (k+1)^rho+l^rho<=(k+l+1)^rho proves the second.

Finally A_mass tends to1, (1+1/m)^m tends to e, and (2/c)^m tends to0.
Equation (1) gives (S_m+1)/lambda^m ->e. Hence the ACTUAL finite
benchmark, without any fitted dyadic constant or phase assumption, obeys

    K B_(2^m-1)
      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2S_m+1) ->2/e.          (27)

Both (5) and (7) imply (8).

## 8. Scope and the next real gap

No external theorem was used as a black box. Exact scalar-order/area
constructions and the earlier fixed13/5 clock arguments informed this
choice of family, but every estimate on the actual near-critical word
has been derived here, with uniform constants.

The restrictions are substantive: a common quadratic neighborhood;
outer logarithmic slope at most3/4 in the reciprocal case, or2/3 in
the small-curvature nonresonant case; a specified ray-comparison bound;
and, for the latter case, mu<=1/d_2. General nonresonant curvatures
above1/d_2, independent core curvatures, outer ramps with logarithmic
slope near or above1, or profiles without a common quadratic core are
not covered. No upper bound on all scalar functions, no unrestricted
W_n upper bound, and no completion of the project target is asserted.

There is an exact countertest against silently removing resonance from
the annihilation conclusion. Take mu=1, G_+=G_-=min(t,1), R=1, and
epsilon=[2 product_(h in H_m)(1+h)]^(-1)>0 as the initial point.
Every prefix radius is below1/2, by induction using the bound1+h for
each update, so the whole trajectory uses g(x)=x. All h=d_j exceed1;
therefore every update strictly crosses zero and none reaches it.
Its final normalized objective is the strictly positive quantity

    product_(h in H_m)(h-1)^2
      =(ac)^(2(2^m-1)) c^(-2m).

For the last identity, the multiplicity of d_j is2^(m-j-1), the total
is n=2^m-1, and the multiplicity-weighted sum of j is n-m (induction
in m verifies this identity). This refutes the extended annihilation
claim, not an objective bound at K. Also S_1/d_1<3/4 and
S_2/d_2<3/2 on the actual parameter range, so the uniform7/4 waiting
budget used in Section5 cannot be imported to the first three levels.
The latter strict inequality follows from
S_2-(3/2)d_2=3/2+a(2+c-(3/2)c^2)<0 using a>2/5,c>12/5.
